  |<!DOCTYPE html>
  |<html>
  |<head>
  |<meta charset="utf-8">
  |<title>Getting Started with the k13pkg Python Package</title>
  |</head>
  |<body>
  |<h1>Getting Started with the k13pkg Python Package</h1>
  |<ol>
  |<li>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime\win64 folder on the PATH.</li>
  |<li>Install the package from this folder with <code>python -m pip install .</code>, or put this folder on PYTHONPATH.</li>
  |<li>In Python, import the package, then the matlab module, and call the functions through the handle <code>initialize()</code> answers:
  |<pre>import k13pkg
  |import matlab
  |handle = k13pkg.initialize()
  |result = handle.functionName(matlab.double([1, 2, 3]))
  |handle.terminate()</pre></li>
  |<li>Run the program: <pre>python driverApplication.py</pre></li>
  |</ol>
  |</body>
  |</html>

main.file.readme.txt	1441 chars, CRLF 35, ends with newline 1
  |k13pkg MATLAB Python Package
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed: the package finds it through the
  |JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT environment variable, the registry key its installer writes, or its runtime\win64
  |folder on the PATH, and runs its functions there. The JGraph Runtime holds the matlab module the
  |package's functions take and give arrays of.
  |
  |Verify that a Windows version of Python 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and/or 3.12 is installed. A newer
  |Python is warned about and used.
  |
  |2. Installing the k13pkg Package
  |
  |A. Change to the directory that contains the file setup.py and the subdirectory k13pkg.
  |If you do not have write permissions, copy all its contents to a temporary location and
  |change to that directory.
  |
  |B. Execute the command:
  |
  |    python -m pip install . [options]
  |
  |Use --user to install to your home folder, or --prefix="installdir" to install to
  |"installdir" and add "installdir" to the PYTHONPATH environment variable. A program can also
  |put this folder on its PYTHONPATH and import the package without installing it.
  |
  |3. Using the k13pkg Package
  |
  |The k13pkg package is on your Python path. To import it into a Python script or session,
  |execute:
  |
  |    import k13pkg
  |
  |Import the package before the matlab module: it puts the JGraph Runtime's matlab module on the
  |path. initialize() answers a handle, whose methods are the package's MATLAB functions.

  |# Installs the Python package k13pkg, generated by JGraph's mcc:
  |#     python -m pip install .
  |# from the folder this file is in.
  |
  |try:
  |    from setuptools import setup
  |except ImportError:
  |    raise EnvironmentError("Installation failed. Install setuptools using 'python -m pip install setuptools', then try again.")
  |
  |if __name__ == '__main__':
  |    setup(
  |        name='k13pkg-R2025b',
  |        version='25.2',
  |        description='A Python interface to k13pkg',
  |        platforms=['Windows'],
  |        packages=[
  |            'k13pkg'
  |        ],
  |        package_data={'k13pkg': ['*.ctf']},
  |    )

main.file.k13pkg\__init__.py	7126 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package k13pkg of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13pkg'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

dotted.init.org\k13\deep\__init__.py	7138 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package org.k13.deep of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'org.k13.deep'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

underscore.init._k13u\__init__.py	7124 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package _k13u of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = '_k13u'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |

handleonly.init.k13h\__init__.py	7122 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package k13h of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13h'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

  |build-backend = "setuptools.build_meta"

  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

varargs.init.k13v\__init__.py	7122 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package k13v of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13v'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

script.init.k13sc\__init__.py	7124 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package k13sc of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13sc'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location

builtinname.init.json\__init__.py	7122 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package json of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'json'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

  |<head>
  |<meta charset="utf-8">
  |<title>Getting Started with the k13s Python Package</title>
  |</head>
  |<body>
  |<h1>Getting Started with the k13s Python Package</h1>
  |<ol>
  |<li>Install the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3, or set JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime\win64 folder on the PATH.</li>
  |<li>Install the package from this folder with <code>python -m pip install .</code>, or put this folder on PYTHONPATH.</li>
  |<li>In Python, import the package, then the matlab module, and call the functions through the handle <code>initialize()</code> answers:
  |<pre>import k13s
  |import matlab
  |handle = k13s.initialize()
  |result = handle.functionName(matlab.double([1, 2, 3]))
  |handle.terminate()</pre></li>
  |<li>Run the program: <pre>python driverApplication.py</pre></li>
  |</ol>
  |</body>
  |</html>

samples.file.readme.txt	1429 chars, CRLF 35, ends with newline 1
  |k13s MATLAB Python Package
  |
  |1. Prerequisites for Deployment 
  |
  |Verify that the JGraph Runtime (0.2.3) is installed: the package finds it through the
  |JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT environment variable, the registry key its installer writes, or its runtime\win64
  |folder on the PATH, and runs its functions there. The JGraph Runtime holds the matlab module the
  |package's functions take and give arrays of.
  |
  |Verify that a Windows version of Python 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and/or 3.12 is installed. A newer
  |Python is warned about and used.
  |
  |2. Installing the k13s Package
  |
  |A. Change to the directory that contains the file setup.py and the subdirectory k13s.
  |If you do not have write permissions, copy all its contents to a temporary location and
  |change to that directory.
  |
  |B. Execute the command:
  |
  |    python -m pip install . [options]
  |
  |Use --user to install to your home folder, or --prefix="installdir" to install to
  |"installdir" and add "installdir" to the PYTHONPATH environment variable. A program can also
  |put this folder on its PYTHONPATH and import the package without installing it.
  |
  |3. Using the k13s Package
  |
  |The k13s package is on your Python path. To import it into a Python script or session,
  |execute:
  |
  |    import k13s
  |
  |Import the package before the matlab module: it puts the JGraph Runtime's matlab module on the
  |path. initialize() answers a handle, whose methods are the package's MATLAB functions.

  |        name='k13s-R2025b',

  |# The Python package k13m of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.

  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13m'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()

mcc_version.init.k13vr\__init__.py	7124 chars, CRLF 162, ends with newline 1
  |# The Python package k13vr of deployed MATLAB functions, generated by JGraph 0.2.3's mcc.
  |# It runs on the JGraph Runtime 0.2.3: the folder JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT names, else the one its
  |# installer registered, else the one whose runtime\win64 folder, where jgrt.dll is, is on the PATH.
  |# Import it before the matlab module, which comes from that runtime.
  |""" Package for executing deployed MATLAB functions """
  |
  |import atexit
  |import importlib
  |import json
  |import os
  |import sys
  |import warnings
  |import weakref
  |
  |
  |class _PathInitializer(object):
  |    SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS = ['3_9', '3_10', '3_11', '3_12']
  |    RUNTIME_VERSION_W_DOTS = '25.2'
  |    JGRAPH_VERSION = '0.2.3'
  |    PACKAGE_NAME = 'k13vr'
  |
  |    def __init__(self):
  |        self.interpreter_version = self.check_interpreter_version()
  |        self.runtime_root, self.python_dir, self.bridge_path = self.find_runtime()
  |        self.mr_handle = None
  |        self.ml_handle = None
  |        self.instances_of_this_package = set()
  |
  |    def check_interpreter_version(self):
  |        # The versions R2025b's packages take; a newer one is warned about and taken.
  |        ver = sys.version_info
  |        version = '{0}_{1}'.format(ver[0], ver[1])
  |        version_with_dot = version.replace('_', '.')
  |        if version in self.SUPPORTED_PYTHON_VERSIONS:
  |            return version
  |        if ver[1] > 12:
  |            warnings.warn('Python versions 3.9, 3.10, 3.11, and 3.12 are supported, but your version of Python is %s' % version_with_dot)
  |            return version
  |        raise EnvironmentError('Python {0} is not supported.'.format(version_with_dot))
  |
  |    def find_runtime(self):
  |        # The runtime's root, its python folder and its jgrt.dll, from the first place that has
  |        # this version's: a runtime of another version is passed over, and named if none is found.
  |        passed = []
  |        for folder, where in self.candidates():
  |            found = self.runtime_at(folder)
  |            if found is None:
  |                continue
  |            root, python_dir, bridge, version = found
  |            if version is not None and version != self.JGRAPH_VERSION:
  |                passed.append('{0} (from {1}) is the JGraph Runtime {2}'.format(root, where, version))
  |                continue
  |            return root, python_dir, bridge
  |        message = ('Could not find the JGraph Runtime {0}, which the package {1} runs on. Install it, set '
  |                   'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT to its folder, or put its runtime{2}win64 folder on the PATH.').format(
  |                       self.JGRAPH_VERSION, self.PACKAGE_NAME, os.sep)
  |        if passed:
  |            message += ' ' + '; '.join(passed) + '.'
  |        raise RuntimeError(message)
  |
  |    def candidates(self):
  |        root = os.environ.get('JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT')
  |        if root:
  |            yield root, 'JGRAPH_RUNTIME_ROOT'
  |        for location in self.registered():
  |            yield location, 'the registry'
  |        for folder in os.environ.get('PATH', '').split(os.pathsep):
  |            if folder and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')):
  |                yield folder, 'the PATH'
  |
  |    def registered(self):
  |        # The runtime's installer registers each version under the machine's key or the user's.
  |        try:
  |            import winreg
  |        except ImportError:
  |            return
  |        for hive in (winreg.HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, winreg.HKEY_CURRENT_USER):
  |            try:
  |                with winreg.OpenKey(hive, 'SOFTWARE\\JGraph\\JGraph Runtime\\' + self.JGRAPH_VERSION) as key:
  |                    location = winreg.QueryValueEx(key, 'Location')[0]
  |            except OSError:
  |                continue
  |            if location:
  |                yield location
  |
  |    @staticmethod
  |    def runtime_at(folder):
  |        # A runtime's root or its runtime\win64 folder (the root's marker names its version), or
  |        # a folder holding the engine, jgrt.dll beside it and the python folder, as JGraph's own does.
  |        folder = os.path.normpath(folder)
  |        for root, bridge in ((folder, os.path.join(folder, 'runtime', 'win64', 'jgrt.dll')),
  |                             (os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(folder)), os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll'))):
  |            marker = os.path.join(root, 'jgraph-runtime.json')
  |            if os.path.isfile(marker) and os.path.isfile(bridge):
  |                try:
  |                    with open(marker, encoding='utf-8') as text:
  |                        version = json.load(text).get('version')
  |                except (OSError, ValueError):
  |                    continue
  |                return root, os.path.join(root, 'python'), bridge, version
  |        bridge = os.path.join(folder, 'jgrt.dll')
  |        if os.path.isfile(bridge) and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(folder, 'python', 'matlab_pysdk')):
  |            return folder, os.path.join(folder, 'python'), bridge, None
  |        return None
  |
  |    def update_paths(self):
  |        if self.python_dir not in sys.path:
  |            sys.path.insert(0, self.python_dir)
  |
  |    def import_matlab_pysdk_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab_pysdk.runtime')
  |        self.mr_handle._attach(self.bridge_path, self.JGRAPH_VERSION)
  |
  |    def import_matlab(self):
  |        self.ml_handle = importlib.import_module('matlab')
  |
  |    def initialize_package(self):
  |        package_handle = self.mr_handle.DeployablePackage(self, self.PACKAGE_NAME, __file__)
  |        self.instances_of_this_package.add(weakref.ref(package_handle))
  |        package_handle.initialize()
  |        return package_handle
  |
  |    def initialize_runtime(self, option_list):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().initialize_application(option_list)
  |
  |    def terminate_runtime(self):
  |        self.mr_handle._bridge().terminate_application()
  |
  |
  |# An exception here, such as no runtime found, is the import's.
  |_pir = _PathInitializer()
  |_pir.update_paths()
  |_pir.import_matlab_pysdk_runtime()
  |_pir.import_matlab()
  |
  |
  |def initialize():
  |    # Opens the package's functions in the JGraph Runtime, which starts with no options if
  |    # initialize_runtime() has not started it, and answers the handle to call them on; terminate(),
  |    # exit() and quit() on it close them, as the end of the program does.
  |    return _pir.initialize_package()
  |
  |
  |def initialize_runtime(option_list):
  |    # Starts the JGraph Runtime with options, a list or tuple of strings (-nojvm, -nodisplay), for
  |    # every package in the process; only before the first initialize().
  |    if option_list:
  |        if not isinstance(option_list, list) and not isinstance(option_list, tuple):
  |            raise SyntaxError('initialize_runtime takes a list or tuple of strings.')
  |    _pir.initialize_runtime(option_list)
  |
  |
  |def terminate_runtime():
  |    # Ends the JGraph Runtime: no package in this process can call it afterwards.
  |    _pir.terminate_runtime()
  |
  |
  |@atexit.register
  |def __exit_packages():
  |    for package in list(_pir.instances_of_this_package):
  |        if package() is not None:
  |            package().terminate()
